Each item is also reached through its legend row and its table button, which open the same panel and meet 24 by 24 pixels within FR-94's 480-unit scope (D-71); that is 2.5.8's Equivalent exception, and `scripts/a11y-audit.mjs` checks both. 2.5.8 is a WCAG 2.2 criterion, beside NFR-1's 2.1 AA claim. Rejected: keeping marker centres at least twice the hit radius (36 units) apart. That would be a layout constraint on top of FR-89's capacity, and it would push crowded bands into overflow to make one of three equivalent targets pass alone. Capping each hit radius at half the distance to its nearest neighbour was rejected too: it fixes the fixture but would give about 21 pixels at FR-94's least pixel density (a 480-unit document at 320 pixels). **Cost, accepted:** on a crowded band, a pointer user near two markers has a smaller target on the side they share. |
| **D-82** | **The cross-platform half of SC-8 is checked from 1.0.x** (SC-8, NFR-4, NFR-13, NFR-17). | *Added by the maintainer's ruling on crumb `rg-isw`.* Architecture §10 checks SC-8's cross-platform half with a CI matrix that compares hashes between jobs, and GitHub Actions is blocked by billing (crumb `rg-3wv`). 1.0.0 tests the same-machine half across separate processes (`tests/determinism.rs`). The cross-platform guarantee is designed in, not yet observed: NFR-17 fixes rounding, the decimal separator, line endings and the byte-order mark. What waits for 1.0.x is the evidence. The 1.0.0 release notes will say so. Rejected: comparing hashes by hand on three machines, which proves one commit, while the CI job proves every later one. **Cost, accepted:** a platform difference that NFR-17 missed would reach a 1.0.0 user unseen. |
| **D-83** | **The starter CSV is written by the tool, not shipped beside it** (FR-99, FR-60, NFR-17). | *Added by the maintainer's request on crumb `rg-l4h`, ruled into 1.0.0 on 2026-10-15.* A new author should not have to guess the columns from §6.1. The maintainer chose a command over a file in the release archive: the template then always matches the binary that will read it, there is nothing extra to download, and a test renders it with no warning, so it cannot drift from the parser. It refuses to replace a file because the likeliest existing file at that path is the author's radar. Rejected: a byte-order mark to help Excel. NFR-17 never writes one, and the template is ASCII, so Excel opens it correctly; if an author adds other characters and Excel saves in a legacy code page, FR-61's error says to save as CSV UTF-8. Also rejected: letting `--rings` shape the examples, which no one has asked for. **Cost, accepted:** one more flag, and FR-60's positional argument is required only when the flag is absent. |
| **D-84** | **SC-16, a released binary on a clean machine, is met from 1.0.x** (SC-16, G-8, NFR-2, NFR-3, NFR-13). | *Added by the maintainer's ruling on 2026-10-15, from the PR 15c reviews.* Architecture §10 makes SC-16 a CI job that builds release binaries for each platform NFR-13 names and runs one on a clean machine, and GitHub Actions is blocked by billing (crumb `rg-3wv`). `just publish` creates the release without attaching binaries. 1.0.0 is therefore installed from source with `cargo install --locked`, which needs a Rust toolchain (on Windows, the MSVC build tools too); the binary that produces is still one file with no runtime (NFR-2), invoked from a shell (NFR-3), but the criterion's clean machine is not tested. The 1.0.0 release notes will say so. Rejected: building and attaching binaries by hand for 1.0.0, which proves one release, while the CI job proves every later one. **Cost, accepted:** an author without Rust cannot install 1.0.0. |
| **D-85** | **The wide radar fills the window; the title, key and legend keep their size** (FR-51, FR-69, NFR-1). | *Added by the maintainer's request on 2026-10-15, crumb `rg-18x`, and reworked the same day, crumb `rg-swu`, after its first form proved unusable.* The wide SVG was capped at its own width, one CSS pixel per unit, so a 1850-pixel window drew a 1025-pixel radar between two bands of white space. **The first form** let the whole wide SVG grow, `max(Wpx, calc(100vh × W / H))`; since the legend, key and title were in the same SVG, they grew with it, to about 1.6× on the maintainer's 11-item radar, which left no room and was not usable. **The form adopted** draws the wide arrangement in HTML as four SVGs, cut from the one document at the radar's right edge and the key's rule: a title spanning the width, the radar, the key under it, and the legend beside it *(since v0.93 the title's SVG is cut as wide as the radar part, which holds it, rather than the whole document: D-97)*. A CSS grid gives the radar a column `minmax(0, max(0.8 R px, calc((100vh − T − C) × R / Hr)))` wide, for a radar part `R` × `Hr` under a title `T` tall and page controls `C` tall, and the legend a column of its own natural width. Only the radar fills its column. The legend draws at one pixel per unit in a column as wide as it and a reserved scrollbar gutter, so a classic scrollbar never narrows it; the key draws at one pixel per unit at most, and shrinks with the radar's column when it is narrower than the radar's own width, as between the breakpoint and the natural width; the title did too until v0.93, which cuts its part as wide as the radar part, so it shrinks only where the whole arrangement is narrower than that (D-97). The legend's box is taken out of the grid's sizing and scrolls, so it is exactly as tall as the radar: a long legend scrolls beside a radar that stays in view, and choosing its 40th row leaves the marker it selects on screen. While the detail panel is open the page script pads the box by as much of it as the panel covers, in scroll padding and in real padding, so a focused row is scrolled within the box above the panel rather than the page moving and taking the radar with it (WCAG 2.4.11). The box is out of the tab order (`tabindex="-1"`): its rows are focusable and scroll it, and a browser that stops on scroll containers would otherwise stop on it unnamed. **The floor** is 0.8 of the radar's own width, where its text draws as small as the whole document did at the old breakpoint: FR-69's breakpoint reads only the width, so without a floor a short window could shrink the radar's text past it. **The breakpoint** moves to where the radar reaches that 0.8 beside a natural-size legend, `W − 0.2 × R`, about 70 pixels higher for the PRD example: the legend no longer shrinks to absorb the difference. The page controls, detail panel and table share the radar's and legend's columns as their cap, so their edges stay with the arrangement's. PNG and SVG keep the one document. Reading order is unchanged: title, radar, key, legend. **Cost, accepted:** none for the page controls since rg-247: they sit above the title, and the height term subtracts their height, `3.05rem + 2px` (an icon button and its margin), where they show: once the script runs, on screen, on a page with a control to show. Without script, in print, and on a `--palette` page with nothing plotted no control shows, so `C` is zero and the radar reaches the window's bottom *(since v0.98, rg-ft5; before, it stopped about 51 pixels short there)*. It was first left out, the radar's last 50 or so pixels starting below the fold, because the controls held text whose height text spacing (D-78) changes; since rg-1ru they are icon buttons with no line of text, so their height is fixed in rem. The page's inset is a fixed 20 pixels, so above one pixel per unit its edges sit a few pixels outside the radar's. The wide arrangement switches to the narrow one about 70 pixels sooner. Choosing a marker highlights its legend row, which may be scrolled out of sight in the box; the panel names the item either way. On a wide, tall window the radar's column grows past the radar's own width while the key keeps its own, so the key's rule no longer spans the radar's ink. Each part is its own unnamed SVG, which some screen readers announce as a graphic or group of its own; the release's manual check covers it. With classic scrollbars the legend's reserved gutter, about 15 pixels, is not in the breakpoint or the page controls' cap: at the breakpoint the radar draws a little under 0.8 (about 0.75 with the page's own scrollbar, still above FR-94's 2/3), and the controls stand that much inside the legend's outer edge. With the panel open and the legend scrolled to its end, scrolling the page can shift the legend's rows a little as its padding follows the panel. **Rejected:** CSS zoom by the viewer, which enlarges without adding room; scaling the whole wide SVG, the first form, for the reason above; filling the width alone, which on a wide window draws a radar taller than the screen; a multi-column legend, which a natural-size legend beside a window-filling radar has no room for; and scrolling to the marker when a legend row is chosen, which moves the page away from the row just chosen. This adds no capacity: a crowded band is as crowded at any size, which is D-86's to answer. |
| **D-86** | **The radar grows to fit its markers, in steps, as far as a laptop allows** (FR-32, FR-33, FR-70, FR-89, D-47, D-48, D-85). | *Added by the maintainer's choice on 2026-10-15, crumb `rg-06b`, after D-85 alone left crowded bands overlapping.* The radar's geometry (rings, dividers, label anchors, marker positions and the 660-unit floor) is multiplied by a growth factor `g`; markers, their numbers, strokes, label text and hit areas keep their natural size, so markers move apart. Every FR-33 position is proportional to the radius, so `g` has a closed form: the closest distance between any two markers at natural size, `d`, needs `g = 2 × 10 / d`, taken up to the next of the steps 1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75 and 2, and exactly 1 when every pair already clears. **Any two markers, not two in one band:** at eight quadrants one item in each innermost band fits its band, but neighbours across a divider are 18.9 units apart. **The bound is the screen, not the document.** FR-69's breakpoint is `W − 0.2 × R` pixels for a wide document `W` wide whose radar part is `R` (D-85), so growth raises it; the radar takes the largest step up to what it needs at which the breakpoint stays at or below 1440 pixels, measured on the placed document because labels and the legend add width that does not grow. Ordinary names reach 2× within it; four quadrant names of 30 × "W" hold it to 1.25. **D-48's two reasons, answered.** *Conservation:* D-85 now fills the window, and PNG and SVG default to the document's own width, which grows; only an explicit `--width` holds the output size, where the old trade-off applies, accepted. *Comparability:* `g` moves in quarter steps, so an edition redraws every marker only when it crosses a step, not with every item added. **The narrow arrangement is unchanged**: D-70's 0.3 floor is on the drawn geometry, so a grown radar scales down to 0.3 / `g` and a phone draws it at the same size an ungrown radar would be drawn, overlap included, as FR-89 already allows. FR-89's capacity is evaluated at `g`, so only bands still overfull are reported. **Costs, accepted:** the innermost band, whose tracks are shortest, sets the whole radar's size, so five items in one quadrant's innermost band double the radar while its outer bands stay sparse; crossing a step redraws every marker; on a screen where D-85's height-filling cap binds, a grown radar is drawn smaller to fit, down to 0.8 of its size, and below that needs scrolling, so its markers are no larger on screen than an ungrown radar's would be, while the legend keeps its size either way; FR-40's status rings are still outside FR-89's clearance, as before; and growth counts pairs across dividers and rings while FR-89 warns per band, so at many quadrants or rings a radar can grow to 2× for a cross-divider pair and still overlap there with no warning, which is no worse than before. **Rejected:** uncapped growth, which pushes the breakpoint past common laptop widths so they get the narrow arrangement, which growth does not help; growth sized per band, which D-47 already rejects; growth as a continuous factor, which moves every marker with every item added; and a third radial track, which moves every marker on every radar. *(Since v0.90, a breakpoint that labels alone hold past 1440 pixels warns: FR-105, D-94.)* |
| **D-87** | **The static outputs have a ground; the PNG has a clamped default width, a small-text warning, an area cap and a golden tolerance** (FR-55, FR-57, FR-64, NFR-17, SC-11, SC-18). | *Added by the PR 16 plan; confirmed by the maintainer 2026-10-16.* **Ground:** the documents draw no background, so the static SVG, and the PNG drawn from it, open with a rect of the theme's `Surface1` covering the viewBox; a transparent dark export would put pale ink on whatever slide it lands on. `Surface1` is the only correct value: `MarkerHollow`, the text halos and the solid marker's separating stroke are `Surface1`, and FR-82's contrast is measured against it, so changing it means re-verifying FR-82. **Default width:** FR-57's default is the document's own width, which since 1.0.1 can pass 10,000 units; such a default is drawn at FR-64's 10,000 with a warning naming the natural width, since refusing valid input for a layout limit is what D-71 rejects. An explicit `--width` is never clamped. **Small text:** when the PNG's scale falls below `WIDE_MIN_SCALE`, where legend names draw under `MIN_LEGIBLE_PX`, the run warns and names the least width that keeps them legible. **Area cap:** FR-64 bounds the width, not the height, and a long legend makes the document tall, so an image past 100 megapixels (FR-64's 10,000 squared) is refused before it is allocated, with an error suggesting a smaller `--width`. Peak memory at the cap is about 1 GB: the pixmap, a demultiplied copy for encoding, and the encoder's buffers. The cap bounds pixels, not the cost of parsing and shaping a very large document, which HTML pays too. **Golden tolerance:** NFR-17 claims no PNG bit-exactness across platforms, and tiny-skia's SIMD paths differ by architecture, so the PNG goldens allow a difference of 2 per channel on at most 0.1% of pixels; run to run on one platform, pixels are identical (NFR-4). **Rejected:** a transparent export; a ground drawn by the PNG emitter alone, which SVG would then re-decide; refusing a default width past 10,000; letting the allocator decide; a 64-megapixel cap, which refuses the PRD example at `--width 10000`; exact PNG goldens across platforms. |
| **D-88** | **The exported SVG outlines its text, names it, and takes its size from `--width` unclamped** (FR-54, FR-57, NFR-1, SC-15, SC-25, §14.1). | *Added by the PR 17 plan, crumb `rg-m2l`; confirmed by the maintainer 2026-10-16.* **Outlines:** every text is drawn as glyph outlines, defined once per face and size in `<defs>` and placed by `<use xlink:href x y>` (architecture §11, `docs/spikes/svg-font.md`): no `<text>`, no `font-family`, no `@font-face`, so other applications draw it as resvg does. `xlink:href` rather than SVG 2's bare `href`, which SVG 1.1 consumers do not read. Glyph ids are local to the file: two exports pasted inline into one page would share them, which the README says. **Size:** `width` and `height` on the root are `--width` and the height in proportion, unrounded, or the document's own size by default; neither is clamped, capped or warned about, because no pixmap is allocated and a vector scales without loss (D-87's limits are the PNG's). The file grows by about 50 bytes per drawn glyph. **Names:** the root carries a `<title>` (the title, or D-75's *Technology Radar*) and a `<desc>` (the radar's group name), and is not `role="img"`, which would flatten its headings and markers. Every filled text outside a control is `role="img"` named by its words — as written, so a token wrapped mid-word reads whole (rg-t3p) — so headings keep their names and labels stay readable; in the HTML, where text stays `<tspan>`s that a heading named from its content would run together at each break, a heading wrapped onto several lines carries its words as `aria-label` instead (rg-g0c), and any other filled text outside a control whose break fell inside a token is `role="img"` named by its words, since a label on a plain `<text>` names a generic node that readers ignore; text wrapped only between words stays text (rg-zl6); halos stay hidden, and a control's own name covers its text. With no title there is no level-1 heading; the root's `<title>` names the document. Author data is read under `xml:lang="en"`, as in the HTML. These names reach a reader who opens the file directly; placed in a page or a slide, the placement's alt text is what is read. NFR-1 stays scoped to the HTML, and text cannot be selected or searched, as the spike decided. **Errors:** with `.svg` written, no extension FR-52 names is deferred, so §14.1's "not supported in this version" message has no trigger and is removed. Ships as 1.2.0. |
| **D-89** | **A run of whitespace inside a value is one space, wherever the value is compared or shown** (FR-76, FR-9, FR-15; D-76 extended). | *Added by the maintainer's ruling on rg-cuj, 2026-10-17.* A wrapped line joins its words with single spaces, so a heading wrapped onto several lines is named `Tool Kit` (D-88, rg-g0c) while the same value unwrapped, in the table and in a legend row's name, kept `Tool  Kit` as typed: one value read two ways, and the accessibility audit counted the row as misplaced. **The quadrant label, the declared ring name and the item name are stored tidied when read** — FR-76's trim, then every run of whitespace (Rust's `char::is_whitespace`, the rule the wrapper splits words by, so including no-break space and U+2028) as one space — and **normalisation compares the tidied value**, so `Tool  Kit` and `Tool Kit` are one quadrant, one ring, or a duplicate name (FR-28), never two that show the same. Format characters inside a value stay, as for the trim (D-61). The other free-text columns stay verbatim, as D-76 decided. Rejected: collapsing only for display, which would draw two quadrants with the same label; and leaving it, which keeps a wrapped label disagreeing with its table cell. |
| **D-90** | **A static output's legend can flow into several columns, by an opt-in flag that breaks only between sectors** (FR-100, FR-35, FR-71, D-85, D-87; FR-91, §6.6 and SC-7 amended). | *Added by the maintainer's ruling on crumb `rg-73h`, 2026-10-16: opt-in, not automatic.* In the wide arrangement the legend is one column beside the radar, so a long legend sets the image's height (D-85, D-87): the PNG comes out tall and narrow with the radar at the top of a mostly empty left side, awkward on a slide, and a very long legend at a large `--width` can reach D-87's pixel cap. **Opt-in** because the default output is what every existing edition renders, and a layout that changed with item count would move an edition's legend between columns from one quarter to the next. **A count, 1–4,** rather than a threshold the author would have to reason about in layout units; four columns beside a radar is typically wider than a 16:9 slide. **Breaks between quadrant groups only**: a quadrant is the legend's unit of reading (FR-35), so splitting one would need a repeated or *continued* heading, which would also read twice in the SVG export's outline (D-88); the cost is that one dominant quadrant limits how much the flag shortens the image. **Shortest tallest column**, by drawn height, with a total tie order, so the choice is deterministic and needs no tuning. **Static outputs only**: the HTML legend already scrolls beside the radar in its own column (D-85), and its narrow arrangement stacks it below, so neither has the problem; SC-7 is amended to allow that one difference. **Cost, accepted:** at a fixed `--width` each added column shrinks the whole image, so D-87's small-text warning fires sooner; the author trades height for scale. Scheduled for a v1.x release after PDF export, which this PRD now specifies (FR-110, D-103); a PDF that exports the wide arrangement honours the flag, as FR-100 now names PDF. Rejected: wrapping automatically past a height ratio (the maintainer's ruling); splitting inside a quadrant with a repeated heading; balancing by item count rather than drawn height, which ignores wrapped names and headings; a fixed second column only, which a 300-item radar would outgrow. |
| **D-91** | **The starter palette is written by the tool, as one set of colours that `--palette` accepts unchanged** (FR-101, FR-81, D-83). | *Added by the maintainer's request while scoping crumb `rg-cdo`, 2026-10-17.* `palette.sample.css` was the only starting point, and it held a light `:root` block and a dark `[data-theme]` block with a comment saying to delete one: not a valid `--palette` file as shipped, and a file the author had to find in the repository rather than get from the binary. The same argument as D-83 applies — a file the tool writes always matches the parser that reads it, and a test proves the round trip. **Light set, dark values in comments**, the maintainer's choice: the file is valid as written, the dark set is one edit per line away, and there is one source for both. **The sample file kept, byte-identical**, rather than deleted: FR-81 and `mockup.html` cite it and `palette-proof.html` carries its values, and a test makes drift impossible. **A guide beside it** (`docs/palette.md`), by the maintainer's request, because eight role names such as `--stroke-divider` and `--marker-hollow` do not say where each colour lands; the guide shows it. Rejected: choosing the set with `--theme`, which would make the flag the one aside that is not given alone; and writing both blocks, which needs the parser to give a second block a meaning (D-92). **Cost, accepted:** a third mode in the usage line. |
| **D-92** | **A `--palette` file has a strict grammar: one `:root` rule, known properties only** (FR-102, FR-86, FR-87). | *Added by the maintainer's ruling while scoping crumb `rg-cdo`, 2026-10-17.* FR-86 fixed the values but not the file. **One rule**, because a file that could hold a second block (`[data-theme="dark"]`, `@media`) would need a rule for which block wins, and either answer lets a value the author wrote be silently ignored. **Unknown properties are errors**, not skipped, so a typo such as `--surface1` is named where it is rather than reported as a missing `--surface-1` elsewhere in the file. **Duplicates are errors** for the same reason: a later declaration overriding an earlier one is CSS's cascade, and here it would hide the value the author meant. This is not a CSS parser and does not claim to accept CSS in general; it accepts the subset FR-101 writes and an author edits. Rejected: scanning for declarations anywhere, which accepts the old two-block sample but lets its dark block override its light one; and accepting unknown properties for files shared with other tooling, which no one has asked for and which costs every typo its clear error. |
| **D-93** | **Warn when a PNG or SVG draws a character its font lacks; leave the HTML silent** (FR-104, A-4, R-7, SC-7). | *Added at the maintainer's request, crumb `rg-o25`, 2026-10-17.* A PNG or SVG draws every text with the bundled Inter subset alone (FR-85, D-68), so a name in Arabic, CJK or emoji draws as missing-glyph boxes, while the HTML page falls back to the reader's system fonts and draws it. That divergence was silent: R-7 recorded it and A-4 assumed it away, so SC-7's parity broke with nothing said. **A warning, not an error:** the static output is still usable, and whether a radar's names may be in a script Inter lacks is the author's call. **Static formats only:** the page draws the text, so a warning there would report a box that is never drawn. **Shaped, not looked up:** the cmap alone would flag a decomposed accent the shaper composes into a glyph the subset has, or U+2126 OHM SIGN, which it draws as omega, so detection shapes the text with one cluster per character and names the character at each `.notdef` glyph. The shaper already hides a default-ignorable character it cannot draw, so a joiner or variation selector is never named, while a visible format character such as U+0600 is. **One face answers for both:** Regular and SemiBold map the same characters to the same glyphs, and a test pins that cmap equality, so a regenerated subset that splits them fails the build rather than the warning; it is the cmaps that are compared, not each face's shaping. **Known narrow gaps:** the SVG is outlined by the same shaper detection uses, but the PNG is shaped by `usvg`, which applies bidi mirroring in right-to-left runs, so a mirrored bracket pair could in principle differ. **Rejected:** a fallback font, which D-68 weighed and declined; and checking the whole drawn document, which finds the boxes but not the cell or flag to point at. |
| **D-94** | **Warn when a laptop gets the narrow arrangement, beside FR-95's warning** (FR-105, FR-51, FR-69, FR-71, FR-95, D-73, D-85, D-86). | *Added at the maintainer's request, crumb `rg-rv8`, from the `rg-tzz` review, 2026-10-17.* A quadrant name or `--rings` entry long enough to push FR-69's breakpoint past 1440 pixels sends 1440-pixel laptops, and every narrower screen, to the narrow arrangement; since D-85 the breakpoint is where the wide radar alone reaches 0.8 of its size (FR-51), so a wide radar raises it. The only warning was FR-95's, which speaks of 320-pixel phones and so understates the effect. **The bound is D-86's 1440 pixels**, where growth already stops, so the warning fires exactly where growth gives up on the wide arrangement, and growth never trips it. **The label named binds the wide radar**, drawn at scale 1, chosen as D-73 chooses; FR-95 measures at the narrow arrangement's scale, where labels sit closer in, so a diagonal label can bind the wide radar while a longer one at 6 o'clock binds the narrow. **Beside FR-95, not replacing it:** each message is true on its own terms, one about which arrangement a laptop shows and the other about target size on a phone, and FR-95 alone fires for many ordinary radars. FR-105 never fires alone: past 1440 the wide radar part is at least about 1280 units, since the legend is capped near 400 (D-56), and drawing the radar at 0.3 rather than 1 moves a label in by at most 0.7 × 300 units a side (the label circle, FR-79), so the narrow document stays at least about 850 units wide, past 480. The author therefore sees both, FR-105 first, since it reports the larger effect. **HTML only:** PNG and SVG draw the wide arrangement at any width (FR-71), so the breakpoint does not concern them. **Rejected:** rewording FR-95 to cover both, which joins two thresholds in one message that is wrong whenever only one is crossed; and a bound below 1440, which would warn about radars D-86 deliberately lets grow to it. |
| **D-95** | **The page's table publishes what the radar draws unless asked for everything** (FR-106, FR-17, FR-18, FR-27, FR-78, D-38, D-80). | *Added at the maintainer's request, crumb `rg-fgf`, from the README rewrite, 2026-10-17.* FR-78 put every CSV row and every named column in the table, and D-80 built the detail panel from the table row, so a CSV that doubles as an internal register — a ticket number, an owner, a cost centre, a row kept for the record — was published whole, while the PNG and SVG left all of it out. **Most authors want the table to show what is on the radar**, and publishing everything should take a deliberate step: a column published by mistake cannot be unpublished. So **`--table radar` is the default**, and `--table all` keeps the old page byte for byte. **The default changes, accepted by the maintainer at one user**: radargen has one user, so the break is released as a minor, by the maintainer's ruling, with the change called out in the release notes. **The columns kept are the ones radargen reads** — FR-5's required columns and §6.1.5's optional ones — so the cut is by meaning, not by name or position, and needs no list. **Rows and columns move together** behind one switch: a per-column list was ruled out by the maintainer, and a second flag for rows alone would let a page publish an unplotted row's panel without its columns, or the reverse, for no case anyone has. **No new warning for what is left out** (since v0.97, except a header that nearly names a known column, FR-108, D-98): the rows already carry FR-17's warning, which now says where the row went and names `--table all` (since v0.92, not a row whose ring `--unplotted-rings` names, which D-96 accepts as a limit); a dropped column is the default working as intended, and a warning on every render of a register CSV would be noise an author learns to ignore. **HTML only:** PNG and SVG never had a table, so `--table all` there warns as `--legend hidden` does (FR-103). **By the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-18 (rg-fgf), a quadrant used only by off-radar rows is still drawn and named** — a sector, its label and an empty legend group, in every format (FR-42) — so its name is published, which is accepted. The empty sector stays on purpose: it shows that the quadrant was considered and that nothing in it is on the radar. **Accepted limit:** `data-row` carries CSV line numbers (architecture §5), so gaps in the numbering reveal that rows were dropped, though not their content. **Rejected:** keeping `all` as the default with `radar` opt-in, the first ruling on the crumb, superseded because it leaves the unsafe choice the silent one. |
| **D-96** | **Name the rings kept off the radar, rather than ignoring unknown rings** (FR-107, FR-17, FR-63, FR-91, NFR-20; D-50 and D-51 revisited). | *Added at the maintainer's request, crumb `rg-4bq`, from the README rewrite discussion, 2026-10-17.* A CSV used as an internal register holds rows in rings such as `In Use` or `Rejected` that are never meant for the radar, which is D-50's second shape. Each render warns about them, in one line since NFR-20. **A blanket switch to ignore unknown rings was rejected**: it would also silence `Adpot`, the typo FR-17's warning exists to catch. **Naming the rings keeps FR-17 catching typos**: a named ring is off the radar on purpose, and any other value outside the declared set still warns. **This revisits D-51's rejection of a targeted `--ignore "In Use"`, on habituation.** Grouping cut forty lines to one, but that one line prints unchanged on every render, for a configuration the author chose and will not change. An author learns to skip a line that never changes, and a typo enumerated in it beside `In Use (40, …)` is skipped with it. So the grouped line does not keep a typo distinguishable in practice, which was D-51's case for it. With the deliberate rings named, a clean run is silent and a typo gets a line of its own. D-51's other two objections: *a lossy operation undocumented* — the flag records the loss in the command or build script, where whoever inherits it reads it, under a name that says what happens to the rows, which a stderr line nobody keeps does not; *ring vocabulary split across two flags* — accepted, and bounded, because a value cannot be in both lists and FR-17's warning names the second flag, so a reader of either learns of both. **D-95 is not why the reversal holds; it makes the loss more complete.** Since D-95 those rows leave the default page as well as the radar. **Accepted limit:** under the default `--table radar`, a row in a named unplotted ring leaves no trace in the output and none on stderr; only the command records it. That is the author's stated intent, and `--table all` lists the rows. **Rulings.** *The default set counts toward the overlap:* its rings are drawn exactly as declared ones are, so naming `Adopt` without `--rings` asks for a ring both drawn and not, and the error says how to get the intent: pass `--rings` without it. Silently dropping the ring from the default set was rejected, because one flag would then edit the other's list. *Duplicates and empty entries are FR-63's errors, with its messages:* a repeat is harmless here, but the two lists parse the same, so an author learns one rule. *An unused name does not warn:* the same command serves every edition, and a register may have no `Rejected` row this time, so a warning would fire on a correct command line. A misspelt name hides nothing, because rows in the ring meant still get FR-17's warning, so the misspelling shows on the next render. *FR-17's message names the flag only in a run not given it*, in the shortest form that says what it does, because the warning is where an author with a deliberate off-radar ring meets the problem. An author already using the flag knows it, and the clause would nudge them toward silencing what is more likely a typo. *Order of the remedies:* the fact, how to plot it, how to keep it off quietly, and `--table all` last, so its privacy clause, which says it *also* publishes every other column, ends the line. *Spelling (OQ-2):* `--unplotted-rings` uses FR-17's and FR-18's own word for the rows. It has no short form, because it is set once per radar, as `--table` is. *`--config`:* the key is `unplotted_rings`, an array as `rings` is (FR-91). The overlap is checked after the merge, across sources, naming both sides' effective sources (FR-107). Accepted gap: the key cannot be cleared from the command line, since `--unplotted-rings ""` is an error. *Considered and rejected:* a third "declared but hidden" state inside `--rings` itself, which would put a non-band into an edge-to-centre band list and change FR-12's grammar. |
| **D-97** | **While the legend is hidden, the page switches arrangements at a second, lower breakpoint** (FR-69, FR-103, FR-105, D-85, D-86). | *Added at the maintainer's request, crumb `rg-dok`, from RG-102's recorded limitation, 2026-10-18.* FR-69's breakpoint is where the wide radar reaches 0.8 of its natural size beside a natural-size legend, `W − 0.2 × R`: 891 pixels for the PRD example. With the legend hidden nothing stands beside the radar, so readers between that width and the radar's own 0.8 got the narrow arrangement where a bigger wide radar would fit. **The same rule, with the legend's width taken out:** `R − 0.2 × R`, rounded, for a radar part `R` wide: 536 pixels for the PRD example, 0.8 × 670. **What else spans the page was checked.** The title's part was cut as wide as the whole document, so between the breakpoints it would have drawn at about half size (536 / 1025 for the PRD example); it is now cut as wide as the radar part, which holds it by construction since the title wraps within it (D-99; it wrapped at the legend column's width, 380, until then), so it reaches 0.8 where the radar does, and with the legend shown it now keeps its natural size down to the radar's width rather than shrinking with the whole document. Counting the title at the document's width instead would have put the PRD example's hidden breakpoint at about 820 pixels, losing most of the gain. The key sat in the radar's column, which the hidden legend's column still narrows; it now spans both columns with the radar, never wider than the radar, so it keeps its width wherever the radar does not grow. The hidden legend keeps its column, so the widest one, 418 units, must fit inside the narrowest hidden breakpoint, 536: a compile-time check, leaving about 118 pixels, room for both classic scrollbars: the page's, which the viewport's width includes, and the legend box's reserved gutter, about 30 together. **How it is written.** A second narrow query, on `screen` and under the script's class and `data-legend="hidden"` only, at a specificity that outranks FR-69's: the wide arrangement by default and the narrow one below the query, as FR-69's default and query are, so the gap at fractional zoom stays on the wide side, where the script's `matchMedia` of the same query also puts it. `<main>` carries it as `data-narrow-query-hidden` beside `data-narrow-query`; the script's breakpoint focus handling listens to both and acts only on the query for the legend's state. **A press can switch arrangements.** On a window between the two breakpoints, hiding the legend switches narrow to wide and showing it switches back. The switch is the stylesheet's, so one press is one switch, with no loop: neither query changes, and a media query's width includes the page's scrollbar, so a scrollbar the page's new height adds or removes cannot move it. From the hidden breakpoint up, where a press can switch, the page keeps its scrollbar's gutter on screen (`scrollbar-gutter: stable`), so such a scrollbar does not move the page sideways either; below it no gutter is reserved, since there the narrow arrangement spans the viewport with no inset, which FR-94's target sizes assume, and a gutter-narrowed narrow arrangement above it still draws at FR-94's least scale. The press handler scrolls the page to keep the button where it was on screen. The button is in the page controls, outside both arrangements, so it keeps focus; where a click does not focus it (WebKit, and Firefox on macOS) and the switch hides what had focus, FR-103's rule moves focus to it. An open panel stays open, its item selected in the arrangement now shown, and closes to that item there. **Accepted, as trades:** at the hidden breakpoint, narrowing the window makes the radar smaller and its text larger, from the wide radar at 536 pixels to the narrow arrangement's 420-unit document, the same trade FR-69 makes at its own breakpoint; and a press between the breakpoints visibly re-lays out the page, its left edge and the radar's size, which the maintainer accepted. Nothing is centred to soften it: RG-102's rule that nothing moves sideways on a press stands wherever the arrangement does not change. A page started with `--legend hidden` between the breakpoints can paint once in the narrow arrangement before the script marks the page and the wide one shows, since the script is the page's one script, last in `<body>`; probed in Chromium from a local file, the script ran before the first paint on every load, so a flash needs a slow network. Marking the page earlier would need a second script, which the page's one constant script (NFR-4, NFR-6) rules out, so it is accepted, as RG-102 already accepts such a page showing its legend until then. **Unchanged:** without script, and in print, the legend shows, so FR-69's breakpoint applies; a page started with `--legend hidden` is in the right arrangement from the moment the script marks the page, which is also when its legend hides; PNG and SVG have no breakpoint; and the detail panel's bottom sheet and side-by-side fields stay keyed to FR-69's breakpoint, since they answer the screen's width, not the arrangement. **FR-105 stays on the legend-shown breakpoint**, and nothing else in it or in D-86 changes: the hidden breakpoint is never above the shown one, so D-86's growth bound holds for it already; the legend shows by default and whenever a reader shows it, so a laptop past FR-69's breakpoint still gets the narrow arrangement then; and the warning's fix, a shorter label, is the same. **Rejected:** a `min-width` override for the wide arrangement alone, which leaves a gap at fractional zoom where the stylesheet and the script disagree; counting the title at the document's width, for the reason above; and moving focus after a press to the same item in the other arrangement, which would take it off the button just pressed. |
| **D-98** | **Warn about a header that nearly names a known column, and about no other dropped column** (FR-108, FR-27, D-95). | *Added at the maintainer's request, crumb `rg-prs`, from the `rg-fgf` architecture review, 2026-10-18.* Before `--table radar` became the default, a misspelt known column — `rationalle`, `descripton` — still showed in the table, so the author noticed; under the default it is an unrecognised column and is dropped without a word, and its content silently leaves the detail panel in every format. **A near-miss hint catches the typo without D-95's noise**: it fires once per header, not per row, and only for a name a known column is a plausible misspelling of, so a register's deliberate extra columns stay silent. **One edit, two only for a longer header and a longer name**: one edit catches a single slip anywhere. Two catch a doubled letter plus one more slip (`rationalee`, `statuses`), which makes a header longer than the name. But two edits that change letters rather than add them reach ordinary words: `state`, a very common register column, is two from `status`, `main` and `remain` are two from `domain`, and `note` is two from `name`. So a header no longer than the name, or a name of four characters or fewer, allows one. **Accepted:** `stats` is one edit from `status`, which no tolerance short of an exact match avoids. **Damerau–Levenshtein**, so `nmae` is one edit, as a typist makes it, not two. **A column the header already has is no candidate**, since the author cannot have meant it; that also keeps `rationalle` beside a real `rationale` silent, and lets `rink` beside a real `ring` suggest `link`. **Ties go to the fewest edits, then a fixed column order**, so the suggestion never depends on anything but the header. **Before FR-5's check**, because a misspelt required column is exactly why that error fires. **The message is true under either `--table`**: `--table all` publishes the column, so it says radargen does not know it, not that it is dropped. **The distance comes from `strsim`**, already built for clap's suggestions. **Rejected:** a warning for every unrecognised column (D-95's noise), a fixed list of common misspellings (it misses the next one), and a flag to silence it (an exact rename does). |
| **D-99** | **The title wraps at the width its arrangement gives it** (FR-65, FR-68, SC-7, D-56, D-86, D-97). | *Added at the maintainer's request, crumb `rg-0ms`, from the `rg-dok` UI review, 2026-10-18.* Since D-97 the title's part is the radar part wide, 670 for the PRD example, but the title still wrapped at the legend column's width, 380, so a long title took three lines with about 390 pixels empty beside it on a large window. **The wide title wraps at the radar part's width less two margins, in every format,** so HTML, PNG and SVG keep the same wrap points (SC-7), and HTML's title part holds it by construction. **The grown width, not the ungrown one:** D-86's growth is settled before the title is wrapped and is the same in every format, since a static output reflows only its legend (FR-100); the ungrown width would leave a grown radar's extra width unused. No width in either arrangement depends on the title, only heights, so wrapping it after the growth is no cycle. **The narrow title wraps at the narrow document's width less two margins**, 380 at its authored 420 units, so it cannot overflow the document FR-94 holds to 480; one measure for both would either overflow the narrow document or give back the wide one's gain. The title is therefore wrapped once per arrangement, as the key's columns are chosen per arrangement (rg-zb9). D-56's one legend wrap is unaffected: its rows are what both arrangements must number and read alike, and the title is one heading whose words are the same either way. The wide measure is never under 380, 670 less 40 for the narrowest radar part (a compile-time check), so a title that fits the legend column wraps as it did before and the same in both arrangements; only a longer title changes. **Rejected:** 380 everywhere, the old rule; one measure, the radar part's, in both arrangements, which overflows the narrow document; and widening the narrow document for the title, which FR-94 rules out. |
| **D-100** | **A config file's format-specific keys are silent where they do not apply** (FR-91, FR-103, FR-106; D-96's habituation argument). | *Added at the maintainer's request, crumb `rg-qag`, from the RG-102 UX review, 2026-10-18.* One `--config` file naturally serves every format a radar is rendered to, so a file holding `legend = "hidden"` for its HTML edition would warn on every PNG, SVG and PDF run, naming `--legend`, a flag the author never typed. A warning that prints unchanged on every render is one an author learns to skip, and it then hides the warnings that matter (D-96). **Silent when the value comes from the file; the flag still warns**, since a flag typed for a PNG is a mistake about that run. **Worded by its source** (`the legend key in radar.toml applies to HTML pages; …`) was considered and rejected: it fixes the misnaming, not the habituation. `theme`, `width` and `legend_columns` were already silent on HTML, so this makes the file's keys consistent in both directions. **Also decided by the maintainer for the config file, 2026-10-18, from the PR 19a review:** (1) **a theme or palette flag wins over the opposing key** (FR-59): `--palette` drops the file's `theme` and `--theme` drops its `palette`, so one file can carry the house theme while a branded edition passes a palette; both keys in the file stay FR-59's error. Refusing the pair across sources, the first design, made any file with `theme` unusable with a palette and broke FR-91's promise that a flag overrides its key. (2) **A path from the file is guarded** (FR-91), since anyone who can edit the repository can edit the file: a palette it names is never quoted in errors, since `palette = "../../.env"` would otherwise print a secret's first 40 characters into a CI log; and a path it names that exists must be a regular file, so a pipe or a terminal cannot hang the run. Confining paths to the file's folder was considered and rejected: a palette shared between radars is a legitimate use. |
| **D-101** | **The column is `sector`, and `quadrant` stays accepted as its former name** (FR-5, FR-27, FR-4, FR-108, FR-99; crumb `rg-k16`). | *Added at the maintainer's decision, 2026-10-18 and 2026-10-22.* A quadrant is a quarter circle, and ThoughtWorks says "quadrant" because its radar always has exactly four; radargen draws one to eight (FR-88 warns above six), so "sector" is the accurate word, and the CSV column, the template (FR-99), every message, the help and the page's text use it. **`quadrant` stays accepted** as the column's name, matched under FR-76 as every header name is, so existing and BYOR-shaped CSVs keep working unmodified; a CSV renders byte for byte the same under either name. **A header holding both is a hard error**, as FR-4's repeated name is, naming both columns (`column 2 (B) 'sector' and column 5 (E) 'Quadrant' both name the sector column (quadrant is its former name); …`); a repeat of one name is still FR-4's own error. **FR-108 knows `quadrant`**, so it never draws a near-miss warning, and a misspelling of it (`quadrnat`) suggests `sector`, the name to write, not the former one. **An exception to FR-27:** the HTML table's heading for this column, and so the detail panel's label, is always `Sector`, even under a `quadrant` header, so the page uses one word throughout. Accepting only `sector` was rejected: it would break every BYOR CSV, which FR-27 promises keeps working. |
| **D-102** | **The starter config is written by the tool, every key commented out** (FR-109, FR-91, D-83, D-91). | *Added at the maintainer's request on crumb `rg-2ss`, 2026-10-18 and 2026-10-22.* FR-91's thirteen keys, their types and their defaults are spread across the help and this document; a file the tool writes lists them all where they are used, and a test ties it to the key list, so it cannot fall behind a new key. **Commented out**, the maintainer's choice: the file changes nothing until a line is uncommented, so writing it is safe and every line is opt-in; a file of live defaults would pin today's defaults into every radar that kept it. **Examples that are valid**, so uncommenting a line never gives a key problem; a test uncomments every line but `palette` or `theme`, which FR-59 lets only one of be set. **`subtitle = "Created {edition}"`** and `edition = "today"`, the maintainer's choice, so `input`, `output`, `edition` and `subtitle` uncommented make a scheduled render with no other argument. **One text**, `docs/product/config.sample.toml`, included into the binary as the palette sample is (D-91). Rejected: a short form, since it is run once per radar, as FR-99's and FR-101's are. |
| **D-103** | **PDF is written directly from the document, its text real text, untagged for now** (FR-110, FR-45, FR-71; D-88, D-75). | *Added from the PR 20 design with the maintainer, 2026-10-22.* **Directly, not through an SVG-to-PDF crate:** the planned route (crumb `rg-5uf`, 2026-10-12) was svg2pdf on the usvg tree the PNG path builds, but svg2pdf 0.13.0 is on usvg 0.45 and krilla-svg 0.8.1 on 0.47, while radargen is on 0.48; a second usvg is a duplicate crate the lint refuses, and the other tree is not ours to hand over. The document already holds everything a PDF needs — shapes, literal colours once a palette binds them, and text the shaper places — so a writer over it with `pdf-writer` is one more emitter beside the SVG converter, deterministic by construction and with no usvg in its path. **Real text, not outlines, except a halo:** a PDF is searched and copied from in a way an SVG opened for editing is not, so D-88's outlining would cost more here than it saves; the shaper still places every glyph, so the layout is the PNG's and the SVG's. The visible text stays text. Only its halo is drawn as stroked glyph outlines: a halo drawn as text duplicated every ring label in copy, search and screen readers, and an outline carries no characters to extract. **Untagged for now:** the radar PDF has a title, a language and text in reading order, but no structure tree; tagging both PDFs together is crumb `rg-ffj`, after PR 21's table, which needs structure most; until then a screen-reader user is better served by the HTML page. `/Lang (en)` is fixed, as the HTML page's `lang` is; a CSV in another language is a known limit for `rg-ffj`. **The page is the document's size**; paper sizes come with the full PDF (crumb `rg-4b8`). Rejected: svg2pdf or krilla-svg on an older resvg (a downgrade the PNG goldens and harfrust's pin would both pay for, `rg-v3a`); outlined text (unsearchable); minimal tagging in this PR (it roughly doubles it, and PR 21 would redo it). |
| **D-104** | **The full PDF reverses D-26 for one output** (FR-112). | *Added from the RG-104 design with the maintainer, 2026-10-22.* Its readers are stakeholders outside the radar team who have a say in, or are affected by, what moves through the rings. They consult rather than glance, scanning for what affects them, so per-item prose reaches a static format, in the full PDF only; the radar PDF, PNG and SVG keep D-26. What is absent — dropped since an earlier edition, or expected and missing — is not shown: a PDF can only show what is on the radar, and a reader with questions asks the radar team. Built on radargen's own PDF writer (D-103): the Fullbleed spike once planned for this (crumb `rg-4b8`) is dropped, since it would bring a second text layout (against D-67) and unknown tagging. `--pdf radar\|full`, not `light\|full`, so the value cannot be read as a colour (renamed by the maintainer, 2026-10-23). |
| **D-105** | **Paper is landscape, Letter or A4, always titled, its legend larger and tighter** (FR-111, FR-65). | *Added from the RG-104 design with the maintainer, 2026-10-22.* Landscape because the table needs the width: at 9 pt a prose column gets about 58 characters a line against 30 in portrait. One orientation throughout, so the PDF prints in one pass. The drawing-sized radar PDF stays the default so a reader can still scale it to print, and `--paper none` restores it over a file's `paper`. A printed page needs a name, so paper draws *Technology Radar* where FR-65 would draw nothing, and the footer carries the subtitle so two editions' pages cannot be mixed. The legend is drawn at 16/15 so its ring headings reach 8 pt, with 26 px rows since paper has no touch target. Accepted: the radar's own ring labels print at 7.5 pt and its marker numbers at about 5.3 pt, the scale the HTML page's narrowest wide arrangement reaches; a manual print check covers them. |
| **D-106** | **A box carries its character** (FR-113). | *Added from the RG-104 design with the maintainer, 2026-10-22.* PDF/UA-1 forbids glyphs that map to no Unicode and the `.notdef` glyph (ISO 14289-1, 7.21.7 and 7.21.8), so FR-104's box becomes a drawn path whose `/ActualText` is the real character. Rejected: claiming PDF/UA only for PDFs with no boxes. Changes FR-110's "a box copies as nothing". |
| **D-107** | **PDF/UA-1 for both PDFs, English assumed** (FR-114). | *Added from the RG-104 design with the maintainer, 2026-10-22.* Chosen though no rule requires it: better to have it and not need it. `/Lang (en)` stays fixed, as the HTML page's `lang` does; a CSV in another language renders with its language misdeclared. |
## 10) Risks and assumptions

### What `mockup.html` validates — and what it doesn't

The prototype in this folder is a **layout validation artefact**, not a reference implementation. Reading it as a specification would import decisions it never made.

**It validates:** the polar geometry at 3, 4, and 5 sectors; equal-width bands at 2, 4, and 5 rings, driven by a ring list rather than a constant (FR-14, FR-32); the divider-at-12-o'clock rule (FR-31); marker distribution within a band (FR-33); a declared ring with no matching rows still drawing its band (FR-19); rows whose ring is outside the declared set leaving the graphic and the numbering while surfacing in the table view (FR-17, FR-18, FR-41); contiguous numbering across plotted items only, assigned in the legend's reading order so a sector's numbers ascend as the reader scans down its group (FR-34, D-33); title block and key inside the shared document, and the title reclaiming its space when omitted (FR-65, FR-66); both arrangements and the media-query switch between them (FR-68–FR-70); the legend authored in SVG within one shared document (FR-44); marker↔legend cross-highlighting on hover and focus (FR-37); selection indicated on marker and legend row alike and distinguishable from hover (FR-72, FR-73); the legend's three indented levels and its inline per-row qualifier and status, with the ring kept in the accessible name (FR-35, FR-74, FR-75); keyboard operability and the detail panel's open/close behaviour (FR-38, FR-48, FR-49); and no horizontal page scroll at 390px (FR-51).

**It does not validate, and must not be copied from:** anything requiring real font metrics — it measures text through the browser's Canvas API, which has no Rust equivalent and is the whole of R-1. It escapes its interpolations (NFR-6), but through one HTML-context helper rather than the per-context escaping three output paths need, so it demonstrates the discipline and not the implementation. It has no CSV parser, so none of §6.1's parsing, validation, or diagnostics behaviour is exercised — the datasets are hand-authored JavaScript objects. It renders only to the DOM, so nothing about PNG, SVG, determinism (NFR-4), or font pinning (NFR-7) is tested. It exposes markers and legend rows as controls but **does not meet FR-50**: its markers are labelled `"N. Name"` with no position, and only legend rows carry the ring (FR-75). Nor is its **label placement** to be copied: it centres every sector name on the label point, which D-70 replaced.

**It is not the source of truth for anything.** Where the prototype and §§3–7 disagree, the requirement wins and the prototype is simply out of date. Two entries came *from* the prototype rather than the other way round — FR-70 and D-31 exist because stacking the full-width document was visibly illegible on a phone once built — which is what the artefact is for, and not a licence to read layout decisions off it.

### What `palette-proof.html` records

A second artefact sits alongside this document: **`palette-proof.html`**, the verification behind FR-81–FR-83. It shows the eight series colours drawn as the radar draws them on both theme grounds, every contrast ratio against every surface, the four vision simulations, and the prefix-quality table FR-83 summarises.

**It is evidence, not specification.** The normative values are FR-81 and FR-82; the proof sheet exists so that a future change to the palette can be re-argued against the same measurements rather than re-derived from scratch, and so the two rejected alternatives in D-41 stay visible instead of being proposed again. Where it and §§6–7 disagree, the requirement wins — the same rule §10 already applies to `mockup.html`. It is a static page with no build step; open it directly.

| ID | Risk | Impact | Response |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **R-1** | **Text measurement without a browser.** The generator must measure rendered text to wrap legend names (FR-46) *and* to size the SVG canvas — `mockup.html`'s `buildDocument` computes the canvas width from `measureCtx.measureText(...)`, a browser Canvas API with no Rust equivalent that comes free. Real font metrics via `fontdb`/`ttf-parser` are needed, and letter-spacing and text-transform must be accounted for (the prototype pads by a 1.2 fudge factor). | **Was high** — largest single source of estimate variance, affecting the two costliest line items in §14. **Now closed**, and the finding lowers rather than raises their risk. | **Retired by the spike** (`spikes/text-metrics/`, FINDINGS.md). Measurement without a browser works and matches `resvg` to 5e-6 px — but only through the shaper, not by summing advances, which is now FR-84 and D-42. The prototype's 1.2 fudge factor is unnecessary and must not be ported. |
| **R-2** | **Font reproducibility.** `system-ui` resolves differently per machine and not at all in a rasteriser, breaking NFR-4 for PNG and potentially changing wrap points between HTML and PNG of the same edition. | **Medium.** Silent divergence between formats, which SC-7 would catch late. | NFR-7 — pin an explicit font, load it explicitly for rasterisation, inline it for HTML. |
| **R-3** | **Legend length at scale.** A mature radar accumulates items; with radar and legend in one document and no inner scroll (D-14), a 100-item edition produces a very tall document and an unwieldy PNG. The wide arrangement (D-16) helps at small item counts, where the legend fits beside the radar — but past roughly 25 items the legend column is the taller of the two and sets the document height on its own, so the problem returns. | **Medium**, and it grows over time rather than appearing at once. | Accepted for this version. Revisit if a published edition becomes awkward to read — likely as a multi-column legend in the wide arrangement, which stays within the one-document constraint and is the natural next step now that the legend has a column of its own. |
| **R-4** | **One-time visible reorder on migration.** D-7 re-sorts sectors alphabetically, so any radar whose previous category order differed shifts once — in the originating case, `Tool, Platform, Practice` becomes `Platform, Practice, Tool`. Readers comparing editions side by side see a change with no underlying change to the standards. | **Low**, but externally visible. | Note it in the release notes of the first edition rendered by this tool. Not a defect. |
| **R-5** | **CSV backfill effort is unquantified.** How much of the existing BYOR-fed CSV needs new optional columns before switching is worth it (OQ-4) is unknown, and it gates SC-3. | **Medium** — it can delay adoption after the tool is finished. | Audit the current CSV against §6.1.5 before implementation starts; it is a data task that can run in parallel. |
| **R-6** | ~~`--palette` specified but undecided.~~ **Closed** — OQ-6 resolved to ship it (D-44), and FR-86/FR-87 give the file the validation rules it was missing. | — | No longer a risk. The flag is normative, in v1.1, with its own success criterion (SC-21). |
| **R-7** | **Font script coverage.** *Licensing half closed:* FR-85 names Inter under OFL-1.1, which grants both the embedding NFR-5 needs and the redistribution NFR-7 needs, so the release blocker is gone. *What remains:* the subset carries Latin, Vietnamese, Greek and Cyrillic (D-68) but no Arabic, Hebrew, CJK or Indic glyphs, so a name in an uncovered script renders as tofu in the PNG and SVG, **with a warning since PRD v0.89 (release 1.7.0)** (FR-104, D-93). | **Low**, reduced from Medium — a wrong-output failure, announced by FR-104 since PRD v0.89 (release 1.7.0), and bounded by A-4 and by an input the maintainer authors. | Recorded as A-4 rather than fixed. Two things to confirm while packaging: the measured subset size, and whether the font declares a **Reserved Font Name** — OFL permits subsetting, but subsetting is *modification*, and an RFN would forbid keeping the original name on the modified file. |
| **R-8** | **Repository size per edition.** UC-1 commits every edition, NFR-5 inlines the font into each HTML file, and FR-69 embeds both arrangements, so each dated edition carries a fresh font blob and roughly twice the graphic markup. | **Low**: on the order of a few hundred KB per edition, which a monthly cadence takes years to make noticeable. | Accepted. A shared font file would break NFR-5's single self-contained file; revisit only if a repository's size becomes a complaint. |

**Assumptions**

- **A-1** A catalogue → CSV export step exists and is manual for now (§4). Nothing in this tool depends on how the CSV is produced.
- **A-2** Item counts stay in the tens, not the hundreds, for the foreseeable future — the basis for §4's "manual checking is sufficient" stance. **It does not cover R-3**, which fires at roughly 25 items — inside "the tens", and plausibly within reach of the originating radar, whose legend v0.7 already measured at 14 items.
- **A-3** Readers open the HTML in a current evergreen browser. No IE/legacy support is specified or tested.
- **A-4** Entry text is in a script the pinned font draws — Latin, Latin Extended and Vietnamese, Greek, or Cyrillic (R-7). When it is not, a PNG, SVG or PDF warns (FR-104); the HTML page falls back to the reader's fonts. Right-to-left scripts such as Arabic and Hebrew, CJK and the Indic scripts are out of scope: each needs a second font, and right-to-left and CJK also need bidirectional layout or line breaking without spaces (D-68).
- **A-5** **Sector counts run three to six in practice**; seven or eight render correctly but read as busy, and beyond eight the **taxonomy is what wants revisiting, not the renderer**. The originating case is three and the ThoughtWorks convention is four, which is the range the geometry is comfortable at: a sector narrows from 120° at N=3 to 45° at N=8 and 30° at N=12, and sector labels crowd a single circle with no collision rule (OQ-14). Colour is **not** the first thing to fail — FR-81's eight entries are deliberate headroom so that even the busy end of the range gets distinct colours, not a statement that eight sectors is fine. The tool renders any N ≥ 1 (G-1, FR-43); above six it says so once (FR-88) and renders anyway.

## 11) Open questions

Two kinds of item sit here and they need different handling, so they are split rather than listed flat. **§11.1 holds product decisions** — questions with more than one defensible answer, where somebody has to choose and either choice leaves the document coherent. **§11.2 holds specification debt** — places where the answer is not in dispute but has never been written down, so the requirement is not yet testable. Debt does not need a decision; it needs an author. §14.0 gates the two differently, and a flat list of fourteen entries hides which is which.

### 11.1 Product decisions

| ID | Question | What it gates |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **OQ-2** | Exact flag spelling and short forms (`--width` vs `-w`). Behaviour is fixed (§6.6); spelling is not. | Nothing. Cosmetic; settle at implementation planning. |
| **OQ-3** | Whether a `--strict` mode should enforce "exactly one Default per domain," given §4 rules policy enforcement out. Revisit if manual checking stops holding up as the catalogue grows. | Nothing in scope — §4 rules it out for this version. A post-v1.1 revisit. |
| **OQ-4** | Migration plan for the existing BYOR-fed CSV — how much backfilling with the new optional columns is needed before the switch is worth making. Gates SC-3; see R-5. | SC-3, and the date BYOR can actually be switched off. Runs in parallel with the build (R-5). |
| **OQ-5** | Whether a **Signal/Telemetry** sector (tracking data points worth standardising the collection of — failed-login counts, support-call duration — rather than data *technology*) gets added to the radar's content. **No tool change either way**; this is a content decision for whoever populates the CSV, not a constraint on the renderer. | Nothing. A content decision for whoever populates the CSV; no tool change either way. |
| **OQ-16** | **Whether the marker is sized in output pixels rather than user units, so `--width` buys room instead of magnification.** *Partly overtaken by D-86, which grows the radar's geometry against a fixed marker up to 2× regardless of `--width`; the figures below are at natural size.* Today the marker is 10 user units against a 260-unit radar, so `--width` scales everything together and a crowded band is equally crowded at any output size — capacity is 28 per sector at eight sectors whether the PNG is 660px or 3840px wide. Holding the marker at a fixed **output** size instead makes capacity linear in `--width`: measured at 10 output px, 28 per sector at 660, **57 at 1280, 88 at 1920, 182 at 3840**, with the dot the same physical size in every one. **Those totals are dominated by the outer ring and oversell the gain**: at eight sectors and 3840px the outermost band holds 83 of the 182 and the innermost holds 8, a ratio near 1:10 that `--width` does not move, since it is set by the radius of FR-33's tracks (about 25 and 44 units against 220 and 239). The **innermost** ring is the number worth quoting, because it is where the constraint binds and, under the default set, it is `Retire`: it goes from 2 items to 8 at four sectors and 1920px, and from 2 to 3 at eight sectors. That is a fourfold gain exactly where it is needed — and it also shows the limit of the idea, since going from eight sectors to four buys more innermost capacity at a fixed 1920px than doubling to 3840px does at eight. A-5's argument, arriving from the other direction. **The case is strongest for an estate radar using the ThoughtWorks ring order**, where `Adopt` means "in service" and accumulates, yet sits in the innermost band at 2 items per sector against the default set's 10 (FR-14). For that combination OQ-16 is closer to a prerequisite than an improvement; for ThoughtWorks' own advice radar, where `Adopt` is a curated shortlist, it is an improvement like any other. **This is not the conservation D-48 rejects** — that argument applies to growing the canvas with the marker fixed in *user* units, where marker size and count trade against each other. Fixing the marker in output units breaks the trade, which is why the idea survives where auto-growth did not. **Four things to close before it can be specified.** (a) **SC-7 conflicts as written**: it requires the wide arrangement identical across HTML, PNG and SVG for one CSV, and a 3840px PNG would show its dots at a different size relative to the radar than the HTML does — it needs a carve-out scoping parity to *equal output width*, in the shape D-26 used for per-item prose. (b) **HTML has no `--width`** (FR-57 ignores it there) and no generation-time output size, so it would keep today's fixed ratio — which widens the very gap (a) opens. (c) **FR-89's overflow threshold becomes width-dependent**, so a band that warns at 660 is comfortable at 3840; defensible, but the warning stops being a property of the CSV alone. (d) **The marker must still hold its number** (FR-34, FR-36): at 182 items per sector the numbers reach four digits, so a fixed floor has to grow with digit count, partly re-coupling size to content. | **v1.1's `--width` behaviour and SC-7's wording.** Nothing in v1.0 if HTML keeps today's ratio per (b). Recommended **yes**, once (a)–(d) are settled. |
| **OQ-17** | **Whether to add `--sectors`, an explicit sector order mirroring `--rings`.** Deferred, not rejected (D-7): it would make ThoughtWorks' sequence expressible and avoid R-4's one-time reorder, at the cost of new validation — what a listed sector with no rows does (an empty sector, by analogy with FR-19) and what a CSV sector missing from the list does (error, warning, or appended alphabetically). The alphabetical default stays for anyone who omits it, so adding it is a minor bump. | Nothing. A post-v1.1 revisit, prompted by a radar whose readers need a non-alphabetical order. |

### 11.2 Specification debt

**All eight are closed** — seven across v0.15 and v0.16, and OQ-9's remaining half in v0.37. Nothing in this subsection is open.

**Closed in v0.37**

| Was | Closed by |
| --- | --- |
| **OQ-9** — what "case-insensitively" and "ordinally" mean above ASCII *(the residue)* | FR-76 naming the folding (Unicode default lowercase mapping) and the order (Unicode code point) once, for every comparison and ordering (D-53) |

The entry as it stood before closing, kept for the analysis:

| ID | Gap | Blocks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ~~**OQ-9**~~ | **What "case-insensitively" and "ordinally" mean above ASCII.** *Platform scope resolved in v0.15 (NFR-13); the byte-identity trade resolved in v0.16 (D-40). What remains:* FR-10 and FR-34 order "case-insensitively, locale-independently" without naming the folding — simple Unicode lowercase and full case folding differ, and neither matches JavaScript's `toLowerCase` exactly. Separately, "ordinally" in FR-9 and FR-34 is undefined above the BMP: the prototype's `<` compares UTF-16 code units, Rust's `str::cmp` compares UTF-8 bytes, and the two **invert** for a supplementary-plane character against one in U+E000–U+FFFF. Either would make two implementations number one CSV differently, which NFR-4 says cannot happen. Crumb `rg-wld` holds the analysis and both candidate resolutions. | **Nothing today.** A-4 bounds entry text to Latin script, so no current input reaches either divergence. It gates the *Rust port of the comparator*, not the layout engine — settle it when the first non-Latin entry appears, or at implementation planning, whichever comes first. |

**Closed in v0.15**

| Was | Closed by |
| --- | --- |
| **OQ-8** — marker geometry and the radar's orientation | FR-77 (start angle, clockwise rotation), FR-33 rewritten with the prototype's distribution, inset and radial-parity rules (D-36) |
| **OQ-10** — failure behaviour and diagnostics | NFR-14 (atomic write), NFR-15 (runtime-failure class), NFR-16 (three-tier batching), NFR-10 (physical line, counted from the file's first line), SC-18 (D-37) |
| **OQ-11** — what the table view contains | FR-78 (every row, every present column, rows activatable), FR-106 (`--table radar` by default, `all` for every row and column), SC-19 (D-38, D-95) |
| **OQ-13** — NFR-1's evidence path | NFR-1 scoped away from `--palette`, FR-50's unreachable escape hatch struck, FR-80 (`<html lang>`), SC-17 |
| **OQ-14** — the wrapping algorithm | FR-79 (greedy first-fit, fixed max column, mid-token break for an overlong token) (D-39) |
| **OQ-15** — NFR-6's output contexts | NFR-6 enumerating all five, including the `</script>` case, and SC-9's fixture extended to reach it |

**Closed in v0.16**

| Was | Closed by |
| --- | --- |
| **OQ-6** — whether `--palette` ships | It ships: FR-86 (every role required), FR-87 (series count free, which is how a radar exceeds FR-83's eight), SC-21 (D-44) |
| **OQ-7** — whether the tool is published | Published, GPL-3.0-or-later: NFR-18 (D-45). The BYOR clone is archived rather than deleted on retirement (§8) |
| **OQ-9** — cross-machine determinism *(byte-identity half)* | NFR-4 split into byte-identity for HTML/SVG and pixel-identity for PNG; NFR-17 fixing rounding, decimal separator, line endings and BOM; SC-8 comparing PNG by decoded pixels (D-40) |
| **OQ-12** — the built-in palette | FR-81 (eight entries, order normative), FR-82 (neutral roles, both themes), FR-83 (the separability bound); `palette.sample.css` shipped; SC-20 (D-41) |

## 12) Success criteria

| ID | Criterion | Traces to |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **SC-1** | A published edition rendered by this tool replaces the BYOR-rendered edition without losing any information BYOR showed; `--table all` publishes every row and column (FR-106). | G-4, D-21 |
| **SC-2** | A three-sector radar — the originating Tool/Platform/Practice case — renders without workarounds, with no padding to four. | G-1, FR-30 |
| **SC-13** | One CSV renders successfully under the default ring set with the flag omitted, under `--rings "Hold, Assess, Trial, Adopt"` — spaces after the commas included — and under a non-four count such as `--rings "Adopt,Retire"`. Each render draws the bands its flag declares, in the declared edge-to-centre order. A row whose ring is absent from **the set in use** leaves the graphic per FR-17, with a warning, rather than failing the render, and surfaces in the table view under `--table all` (FR-106) — `Retire` under the ThoughtWorks set, a locally-tracked `Rejected` under any of them. Radii and item numbers **differ between the three renders** — a ring's band index changes with the list it sits in (FR-32), and unplotting a row renumbers those after it (FR-18, FR-34). That divergence is the expected result, not a defect: what this criterion asserts is that each render is internally correct, not that they agree. | G-2, FR-12–FR-15, FR-17, FR-18, FR-32 |
| **SC-3** | Domain, rationale, last-affirmed, and exit data already in the CSV are visible to a reader without opening the CSV. | G-4, FR-22–FR-25 |
| **SC-14** | Adopt — Default and Adopt — Conditional are told apart on the graphic alone, and a reader who has never seen the CSV can say which is which from the key. | G-3, FR-20, FR-66 |
| **SC-4** | A CSV containing rows outside the core model — a locally-tracked ring like `Rejected`, extra columns nobody asked for — renders successfully rather than failing the edition: by default those rows and columns are left off the page, the rows with FR-17's warning, and under `--table all` they surface in the table view. | G-5, FR-17, FR-18, FR-27, FR-41, FR-106 |
| **SC-5** | Every marker and legend row is reachable and activatable by keyboard alone, with visible focus at every step. | FR-48–FR-50, NFR-1 |
| **SC-12** | Activating a legend row marks its marker, and activating a marker marks its legend row — the same indication either way, distinguishable from hover, and cleared by every close route. | FR-38, FR-72, FR-73 |
| **SC-6** | The HTML output is legible and usable at phone width, unmodified from what gets emailed or committed — specifically, **legend text renders at its authored size rather than scaled down with the radar** (FR-70), and the page does not scroll horizontally. Authored size is in user units: at 320 px a narrow document W units wide renders 12.5-unit legend text at 12.5 × 320 / W px, about 9.5 px at 420 and 8.3 px at 480, and one D-71 widens renders it smaller still. | FR-51, FR-70 |
| **SC-7** | The **wide arrangement** — title block, radar, markers, legend, key — is identical in the HTML output and in PNG and SVG export for one CSV: same items, same positions, same marker treatments, same wrap points. Three things this criterion deliberately does not forbid: per-item prose being HTML-only (§6.3, D-26), the narrow arrangement placing the same blocks differently (FR-68, FR-70), and a static output's legend flowed into columns by `--legend-columns` (FR-100, D-90), which moves the legend's blocks but not its wrap points, numbering or order. What it forbids is the *same arrangement* diverging between formats. A character the bundled font lacks (A-4) is the one divergence it cannot prevent: the HTML draws it in a fallback font, the PNG and SVG as a box, and FR-104 warns. | G-7, FR-44, FR-45, FR-65, FR-66, FR-68, FR-71, FR-100, FR-104, NFR-7 |
| **SC-10** | Each hard error in NFR-9 exits non-zero having written **no output file**, and each warning exits 0 having written one; every message names its line and column (NFR-10) and the messages arrive in a stable order (NFR-11), errors as well as warnings grouped by class (NFR-20). FR-90's removal runs first: a `name` cell holding only U+0001 is the empty-`name` hard error, and `Kafka` followed by U+0001 is reported as a duplicate of `Kafka`. | NFR-8–NFR-11, NFR-16, NFR-20, FR-61–FR-64 |
| **SC-11** | A PNG opened by someone who has never seen the CSV states which radar and which edition it is (FR-65) and lets them work out what a hollow marker and a half-ring mean (FR-66), without recourse to the HTML output. | G-9, FR-65, FR-66 |
| **SC-16** | The released artefact runs on a clean machine with no interpreter, runtime, or package tree installed — one binary, invoked from a shell. *Amended by D-84:* met from 1.0.x; 1.0.0 attaches no binaries. | G-8, NFR-2, NFR-3 |
| **SC-8** | Re-running the tool on an unchanged CSV produces a **byte-identical** HTML file and a **byte-identical** SVG file — on the same machine, and on a machine of each platform NFR-13 names. The PNG is compared by **decoded pixel buffer**, not by hash, and must match exactly; a byte difference in the PNG with identical pixels is a pass, not a failure (NFR-4, D-40). *Amended by D-82:* 1.0.0 tests the same-machine half; the cross-platform half is checked from 1.0.x. | NFR-4, NFR-13, NFR-17 |
| **SC-15** | The render itself makes no network request, and no output references an external resource: the HTML file opens from a local disk path or an email attachment **with no network available** and is complete — no external CSS, JS, or font — and the exported SVG carries no external font reference either. | G-6, NFR-5 |
| **SC-9** | A CSV whose free-text fields contain `<script>`, `&`, `"`, `]]>`, **`</script>`, a lone `<`, and a `\u2028` line separator** renders those characters as literal text in all three outputs, with no markup or script execution. The `</script>` case is called out because it is the one NFR-6 context where HTML entity escaping is the *wrong* escaping and the previous fixture passed without exercising it. | NFR-6 |
| **SC-17** | The HTML output is checked against the WCAG 2.1 AA criteria NFR-1 claims and SC-5 does not reach: text contrast (1.4.3), non-text contrast for markers, ring strokes and focus indicators (1.4.11), target size for every control at the breakpoint and at 320 CSS px (2.5.8, FR-94: legend rows and table buttons by size, markers through the Equivalent exception, D-81), name, role and expanded state of every control (4.1.2, FR-50), reflow at 320 CSS px and at 400% zoom with no two-dimensional scrolling (1.4.10), text resize to 200% (1.4.4), text spacing with 1.4.12's four values applied, in both arrangements (1.4.12, D-78; `just text-spacing` automates it in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, so an engine that keeps optional ligatures under letter-spacing is measured as it draws), and a declared page language (3.1.1, FR-80). Checked under **both built-in themes**; a custom `--palette` is out of scope per NFR-1. The audit includes one dataset whose narrow arrangement D-71 widens past 480 units, recorded as D-71's 2.5.8 exception. | NFR-1, FR-80, FR-51, FR-50, FR-94 |
